### Statistische Methoden (12)
<table fit-page-width="true" header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Kategorie</td>
<td>Titel / Name</td>
<td>Autor / Quelle / Firma</td>
<td>Link</td>
<td>Notiz</td>
<td>Verwendung im Kurs</td>
<td>Status</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Statistische Methoden</td>
<td>Importance-performance analysis (IPA): quadrant grid, crosshair choice and diagonal (iso-rating) line</td>
<td>Method; key comparison source: Donald R. Bacon, International Journal of Market Research 45(1), 1-15, 2003, article</td>
<td>[https://doi.org/10.1177/147078530304500101](https://doi.org/10.1177/147078530304500101)</td>
<td>IPA plots mean attribute importance against mean attribute performance in a 2 x 2 grid (Martilla and James 1977). Bacon compares IPA variants across 15 datasets and shows that the traditional quadrant approach can be misleading and that regression-derived (indirect) importance can also mislead; he identifies a diagonal-line (importance-performance gap) approach as most valid and offers a validity check. Teaching steps: choose attributes, measure importance and performance, decide on crosshairs (scale mid-point vs data means), add the iso-rating line, interpret quadrants.</td>
<td>Lab: students run an IPA on a class survey in Excel/R and compare quadrant vs diagonal interpretation. Access: paid (SAGE), WU-Lizenz prüfen.</td>
<td>neu; in Suchergebnis bestätigt</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Statistische Methoden</td>
<td>Revised IPA with three-factor theory and benchmarking (derived importance, competitor comparison)</td>
<td>Method; key source: Wei-Jaw Deng, Ying-Feng Kuo and Wen-Chin Chen, The Service Industries Journal 28(1), 37-51, 2008, article</td>
<td>[https://doi.org/10.1080/02642060701725412](https://doi.org/10.1080/02642060701725412)</td>
<td>Revised IPA replaces stated importance with implicitly derived importance, accounts for nonlinear (three-factor) effects and adds competitor benchmarking so that the performance axis is relative to a competitor. Related variants: competitive IPA (Taplin 2012), zone-of-tolerance IPA (Chen 2014). Use when the data include overall satisfaction and competitor ratings.</td>
<td>Lab extension after the basic IPA: add a competitor column and a derived-importance axis. Access: paid (Taylor & Francis), WU-Lizenz prüfen.</td>
<td>neu; in Suchergebnis bestätigt</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Statistische Methoden</td>
<td>Penalty-reward contrast analysis (PRCA) and asymmetric impact-performance analysis (AIPA)</td>
<td>Method; review source: Tahir Albayrak and Meltem Caber, Total Quality Management & Business Excellence 24(11-12), 1288-1300, 2013, article</td>
<td>[https://doi.org/10.1080/14783363.2013.776757](https://doi.org/10.1080/14783363.2013.776757)</td>
<td>PRCA (Brandt) regresses overall satisfaction on two dummy variables per attribute (low and high performance) to estimate a penalty and a reward; their relative size classifies attributes as basic (dissatisfier), performance (hybrid) or excitement (satisfier). The review shows inconsistent practice in defining low/high cut-offs, standardised vs unstandardised coefficients and classification rules, proposes a standard procedure, and demonstrates that different choices change results on the same data. AIPA plots the impact asymmetry against performance.</td>
<td>Lab: run PRCA with dummy-coded regression on a satisfaction dataset; follow the standardised steps. Access: paid (Taylor & Francis), WU-Lizenz prüfen.</td>
<td>neu; in Suchergebnis bestätigt</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Statistische Methoden</td>
<td>Impact range-performance analysis (IRPA) and impact-asymmetry analysis (IAA)</td>
<td>Method; key source: Josip Mikulić and Darko Prebežac, Managing Service Quality 18(6), 559-576, 2008, article</td>
<td>[https://doi.org/10.1108/09604520810920068](https://doi.org/10.1108/09604520810920068)</td>
<td>Three-step framework using airport passenger satisfaction data: (1) estimate each attribute's reward and penalty potential, (2) plot the range of impact (RIOS) against performance (IRPA) to prioritise, (3) compute an impact-asymmetry index to classify attributes as frustrators, dissatisfiers, hybrids, satisfiers or delighters (IAA). The paper also argues that direct and indirect 'importance' measure different constructs, a key conceptual point for IPA users.</td>
<td>Advanced lab: compute RIOS and IA from PRCA coefficients and compare with the IPA grid. Access: paid (Emerald), WU-Lizenz prüfen.</td>
<td>neu; in Suchergebnis bestätigt</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Statistische Methoden</td>
<td>Kano questionnaire (functional/dysfunctional question pairs) and attractive vs must-be quality</td>
<td>Method; original source: Noriaki Kano, Nobuhiko Seraku, Fumio Takahashi and Shinichi Tsuji, Hinshitsu (Journal of the Japanese Society for Quality Control) 14(2), 147-156, 1984, article (Japanese)</td>
<td>[https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/quality/14/2/14_KJ00002952366/_article/-char/ja/](https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/quality/14/2/14_KJ00002952366/_article/-char/ja/)</td>
<td>Original paper introducing attractive (excitement), one-dimensional (performance), must-be (basic), indifferent and reverse quality, measured with a pair of questions per feature (how do you feel if the feature is present / absent) and an evaluation table. DOI 10.20684/quality.14.2_147 shown on the J-STAGE page; a PDF link exists but free access was not confirmed. In Japanese, so for teaching use English expositions (Matzler and Hinterhuber 1998; Mikulić and Prebežac 2011) and cite the original.</td>
<td>Lab: students design a 5-feature Kano questionnaire, classify answers with the evaluation table and compute better/worse coefficients. Access: J-STAGE (Japanese; PDF link present, free access not confirmed).</td>
<td>neu; Link geprüft</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Statistische Methoden</td>
<td>Stated vs derived importance (direct ratings vs regression/correlation-based importance; importance grid)</td>
<td>Method; illustrative source: Gregg G. Van Ryzin and Stephen Immerwahr, Public Administration 85(1), 215-226, 2007, article</td>
<td>[https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1467-9299.2007.00641.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1467-9299.2007.00641.x)</td>
<td>Compares an IPA based on explicitly stated importance with one based on importance derived from regression on overall satisfaction (US online citizen panel on local government services). The two approaches lead to different priorities; a simulation shows how much satisfaction would change if top-ranked services were improved under each measure. Clear demonstration for students that 'importance' is a methodological choice. Related: Vavra's importance grid (Matzler and Sauerwein 2002).</td>
<td>Lab: compute both importance measures on the same survey and plot two IPAs side by side. Access: paid (Wiley), WU-Lizenz prüfen.</td>
<td>neu; in Suchergebnis bestätigt</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Statistische Methoden</td>
<td>SERVQUAL (gap scores) vs SERVPERF (performance only)</td>
<td>Method; meta-analytic source: François A. Carrillat, Fernando Jaramillo and Jay P. Mulki, International Journal of Service Industry Management 18(5), 472-490, 2007, article</td>
<td>[https://doi.org/10.1108/09564230710826250](https://doi.org/10.1108/09564230710826250)</td>
<td>SERVQUAL (Parasuraman, Zeithaml and Berry 1988, Journal of Retailing 64(1), 12-40) measures 22 expectation and 22 perception items on five dimensions (tangibles, reliability, responsiveness, assurance, empathy); SERVPERF (Cronin and Taylor 1992) keeps only perceptions. The meta-analysis of 17 studies (42 effect sizes) finds both equally valid predictors of overall service quality; adapting SERVQUAL to the context improves its validity, while SERVPERF does not benefit. Practical rule: SERVPERF for tracking, SERVQUAL gaps for diagnosis.</td>
<td>Lab: short SERVQUAL/SERVPERF survey on a campus service; compute gap scores. Access: paid (Emerald) with open repository copy (Griffith, hdl.handle.net/10072/436842).</td>
<td>neu; in Suchergebnis bestätigt</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Statistische Methoden</td>
<td>Customer satisfaction index models (ACSI/ECSI/EPSI) estimated with PLS-SEM</td>
<td>Method; dataset source: Forrest V. Morgeson III, G. Tomas M. Hult, Udit Sharma and Claes Fornell, Data in Brief 48, 109123, 2023, article</td>
<td>[https://doi.org/10.1016/j.dib.2023.109123](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.dib.2023.109123)</td>
<td>Open sample of ACSI survey data for four industries (processed food, airlines, internet service providers, banks) with the ACSI items for expectations, perceived quality, perceived value, satisfaction, complaints and loyalty, plus a description of the PLS-SEM based ACSI model. Lets students estimate the national-index model themselves (e.g., in SmartPLS or R seminr) and compute an index score on a 0-100 scale.</td>
<td>Lab: estimate the ACSI model on the open data and compare industries. Access: open access (Elsevier Data in Brief, CC BY; data in linked repository).</td>
<td>neu; Link geprüft</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Statistische Methoden</td>
<td>Importance-performance map analysis (IPMA) in PLS-SEM</td>
<td>Method; key source: Christian M. Ringle and Marko Sarstedt, Industrial Management & Data Systems 116(9), 1865-1886, 2016, article</td>
<td>[https://doi.org/10.1108/imds-10-2015-0449](https://doi.org/10.1108/imds-10-2015-0449)</td>
<td>Tutorial on IPMA: uses total effects from a PLS path model as importance and rescaled (0-100) latent-variable scores as performance, at construct and indicator level, with a SmartPLS case. It is the SEM-based successor of IPA used with ACSI/ECSI-type models; the paper notes that IPMA assumes linear relationships (so three-factor nonlinearities are not covered).</td>
<td>Lab for students already using SmartPLS: produce an IPMA for 'satisfaction' as target construct. Access: paid (Emerald), WU-Lizenz prüfen.</td>
<td>neu; in Suchergebnis bestätigt</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Statistische Methoden</td>
<td>Critical incident technique (CIT) for satisfaction research</td>
<td>Method; review source: Dwayne D. Gremler, Journal of Service Research 7(1), 65-89, 2004, article</td>
<td>[https://doi.org/10.1177/1094670504266138](https://doi.org/10.1177/1094670504266138)</td>
<td>Synthesis of 141 CIT studies in service research with guidelines for data collection (eliciting satisfying/dissatisfying incidents), classification, inter-judge reliability and reporting. Event-based alternative to attribute-based surveys; also usable as input for Kano or PRCA classification. Pair with Bitner et al. (1990) as the exemplar study.</td>
<td>Lab: each student collects five incidents, the class codes them with Bitner's scheme and checks inter-coder agreement. Access: paid (SAGE), WU-Lizenz prüfen.</td>
<td>neu; in Suchergebnis bestätigt</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Statistische Methoden</td>
<td>Customer feedback metrics: CSAT (mean, top-2-box), NPS and Customer Effort Score (CES)</td>
<td>Method; comparison source: Evert de Haan, Peter C. Verhoef and Thorsten Wiesel, International Journal of Research in Marketing 32(2), 195-206, 2015, article</td>
<td>[https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijresmar.2015.02.004](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijresmar.2015.02.004)</td>
<td>Compares CSAT, NPS and CES (classified by time focus and by full-scale vs extremes) in predicting actual retention for customers of 93 firms in 18 industries, with multilevel probit models controlling for respondent self-selection. Top-2-box satisfaction predicts retention best overall, extremes beat full scales, the best metric varies by industry, and combining metrics improves prediction.</td>
<td>Core methods reading for the metrics debate; lab on computing each metric from raw survey data. Access: paid (Elsevier); OpenAlex lists free access on ScienceDirect, WU-Lizenz prüfen.</td>
<td>neu; Link geprüft</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Statistische Methoden</td>
<td>IPA based on online reviews (text mining, sentiment analysis, machine-learning importance)</td>
<td>Method; key source: Jian-Wu Bi, Yang Liu, Zhi-Ping Fan and Jin Zhang, Tourism Management 70, 460-478, 2019, article</td>
<td>[https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tourman.2018.09.010](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tourman.2018.09.010)</td>
<td>Three-stage method: (1) LDA topic modelling extracts attributes from online reviews, (2) a support-vector-machine sentiment classifier estimates attribute performance and an ensemble neural network estimates importance from the effect on overall ratings, (3) the IPA plot is built. Case with two five-star hotels shows results comparable to questionnaire-based IPA at lower cost and time. The current state of 'data-driven IPA'; later variants use aspect-based sentiment and SHAP values (e.g., Joung and Kim 2021).</td>
<td>Advanced lab (with Python/R): scrape or use provided review data for a Vienna hotel, run topic modelling and sentiment, and build an IPA. Access: paid (Elsevier), WU-Lizenz prüfen.</td>
<td>neu; Link geprüft</td>
</tr>
</table>
